High-Level Overview\n    11→CanvasChain Symphony is a constellation of **10 Rust micro-services** that collectively operate a modular blockchain-NFT platform for generative and interactive digital art.\n    12→\n    13→* Each “movement” (feature domain) is an **independent service** with a bounded context.  \n    14→* Services communicate via **gRPC** for request/response semantics and a **NATS JetStream** event bus for pub/sub and CQRS projections.  \n    15→* A custom consensus algorithm, **Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI)**, blends stake-weighted VRF elections with curator voting to periodically select a “Composer Node” that may commit a generative-art update.\n    16→\n    17→```\n    18→┌──────────────────┐      gRPC        ┌──────────────────┐\n    19→│  Wallet Service  │◀───────────────▶│  Governance Svc  │\n    20→└──────────────────┘                 └──────────────────┘\n    21→        ▲     ▲                               ▲\n    22→        │     │  Event Bus (NATS JetStream)   │\n    23→        ▼     ▼                               ▼\n    24→┌──────────────────┐      Stream      ┌──────────────────┐\n    25→│  Marketplace Svc │◀───────────────▶│  Minting Svc     │\n    26→└──────────────────┘                 └──────────────────┘\n    27→```\n    28→\n    29→---\n    30→\n    31→## 2. Micro-service Topology\n    32→\n    33→| Service           | Domain                           | Patterns Used                          |\n    34→|-------------------|----------------------------------|----------------------------------------|\n    35→| `wallet`          | Key management, authentication   | Strategy (crypto curves)               |\n    36→| `governance`      | On-chain DAO voting             | State Machine, Observer                |\n    37→| `minting`         | NFT creation / composition       | Factory, Proxy                         |\n    38→| `remix`           | Trait evolution & layering       | State Machine, Observer                |\n    39→| `marketplace`     | Orderbook & royalties            | Event-Driven, Proxy                    |\n    40→| `staking`         | DeFi incentives / PoI weights    | Strategy, Event-Driven                 |\n    41→| `discovery`       | Metadata & search index          | CQRS, Event Sourcing                   |\n    42→| `composer`        | PoI candidate & block producer   | Strategy, State Machine                |\n    43→| `oracle`          | Off-chain randomness / VRF       | Strategy                               |\n    44→| `monitor`         | Telemetry & health               | Observer                               |\n    45→\n    46→---\n    47→\n    48→## 3. Pattern – Pluggable Cryptographic Curves\n   237→\n   238→```rust\n   239→// crates/crypto/src/strategy.rs\n   240→use ed25519_dalek as ed25519;\n   241→use bls_signatures::{PrivateKey as BlsPriv, msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> bool;\n   247→}\n   248→\n   249→pub struct Ed25519Strategy(ed25519::Keypair);\n   250→pub struct BlsStrategy(BlsPriv);\n   251→pub struct DilithiumStrategy(dilithium2::keypair::PublicKey, dilithium2::keypair::SecretKey);\n   252→\n   253→impl SigningStrategy for Ed25519Strategy {\n   254→    fn sign(&self, msg: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {\n   255→        self.0.sign(msg).to_bytes().to_vec()\n   256→    }\n   257→    fn verify(&self, msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> bool {\n   258→        ed25519::Signature::from_bytes(sig)\n   259→            .and_then(|s| self.0.verify(msg, &s).map(|_| ()))\n   260→            .is_ok()\n   261→    }\n   262→}\n   263→\n   264→// Implementations for BlsStrategy and DilithiumStrategy omitted for brevity.\n   265→```\n   266→\n   267→---\n   268→\n   269→## 5. Consensus – Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI)\n   270→\n   271→```rust\n   272→// consensus/src/poi.rs\n   273→use rand_core::OsRng;\n   274→use vrf::{openssl::CipherSuite, VRF};\n   275→\n   276→pub struct Candidate {\n   277→    pub node_id: String,\n   278→    pub stake: u128,\n   279→    pub curator_score: u64,\n   280→}\n   281→\n   282→pub fn elect_composer(candidates: &[Candidate], epoch: u64) -> Option<String> {\n   283→    let total_weight: f64 = candidates\n   284→        .iter()\n   285→        .map(|c| (c.stake as f64) * 0.8 + (c.curator_score as f64) * 0.2)\n   286→        .sum();\n   287→\n   288→    let vrf_seed = derive_epoch_seed(epoch);\n   289→    let mut highest = None;\n   290→    let mut highest_score = 0f64;\n   291→\n   292→    for c in candidates {\n   293→        // VRF output ∈ [0,1)\n   294→        let vrf_out = vrf_draw(&c.node_id, vrf_seed);\n   295→        let weighted = vrf_out * ((c.stake as f64) + 1.0).ln();\n   296→\n   297→        let final_score = weighted / total_weight;\n   298→        if final_score > highest_score {\n   299→            highest_score = final_score;\n   300→            highest = Some(c.node_id.clone());\n   301→        }\n   302→    }\n   303→    highest\n   304→}\n   305→\n   306→fn vrf_draw(node_id: &str, seed: [u8; 32]) -> f64 {\n   307→    let mut csprng = OsRng;\n   308→    let key = CipherSuite::generate_keypair(&mut csprng);\n   309→    let vrf = key\n   310→        .0\n   311→        .prove(&seed)\n   312→        .expect(\"VRF prove failed\")\n   313→        .hash::<sha2::Sha256>();\n   314→    let num = u128::from_le_bytes(vrf[0..16].try_into().unwrap());\n   315→    (num as f64) / (u128::MAX as f64)\n   316→}\n   317→\n   318→fn derive_epoch_seed(epoch: u64) -> [u8; 32] {\n   319→    use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n   320→    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n   321→    hasher.update(epoch.to_le_bytes());\n   322→    hasher.finalize().into()\n   323→}\n   324→```\n   325→\n   326→---\n   327→\n   328→## 6. Error Handling & Observability\n   329→\n   330→* `thiserror` for domain-level error enums.  \n   331→* `anyhow` at service boundaries for rich context.  \n   332→* `tracing` with OpenTelemetry exporters for logs, metrics and spans.  \n   333→* Circuit-breaker middleware on all gRPC clients using `tower::limit::ConcurrencyLimitFuture`.\n   334→\n   335→---\n   336→\n   337→## 7. Security Considerations\n   338→\n   339→1. Hardware-backed key storage (`yubihsm` feature flag).  \n   340→2. Merkle-proof inclusion for off-chain metadata.  \n   341→3. Re-entrancy guards on Ink! contracts via `non_reentrant` macro.  \n   342→4. Formal verification pipeline (K-framework specs in `/formal_specs`).\n   343→\n   344→---\n   345→\n   346→## 8. High-Level Overview\nCanvasChain Symphony is a constellation of **10 Rust micro-services** that collectively operate a modular blockchain-NFT platform for generative and interactive digital art.\n\n* Each “movement” (feature domain) is an **independent service** with a bounded context.  \n* Services communicate via **gRPC** for request/response semantics and a **NATS JetStream** event bus for pub/sub and CQRS projections.  \n* A custom consensus algorithm, **Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI)**, blends stake-weighted VRF elections with curator voting to periodically select a “Composer Node” that may commit a generative-art update.\n\n```\n┌──────────────────┐      gRPC        ┌──────────────────┐\n│  Wallet Service  │◀───────────────▶│  Governance Svc  │\n└──────────────────┘                 └──────────────────┘\n        ▲     ▲                               ▲\n        │     │  Event Bus (NATS JetStream)   │\n        ▼     ▼                               ▼\n┌──────────────────┐      Stream      ┌──────────────────┐\n│  Marketplace Svc │◀───────────────▶│  Minting Svc     │\n└──────────────────┘                 └──────────────────┘\n```\n\n---\n\n## 2. Micro-service Topology\n\n| Service           | Domain                           | Patterns Used                          |\n|-------------------|----------------------------------|----------------------------------------|\n| `wallet`          | Key management, authentication   | Strategy (crypto curves)               |\n| `governance`      | On-chain DAO voting             | State Machine, Observer                |\n| `minting`         | NFT creation / composition       | Factory, Proxy                         |\n| `remix`           | Trait evolution & layering       | State Machine, Observer                |\n| `marketplace`     | Orderbook & royalties            | Event-Driven, Proxy                    |\n| `staking`         | DeFi incentives / PoI weights    | Strategy, Event-Driven                 |\n| `discovery`       | Metadata & search index          | CQRS, Event Sourcing                   |\n| `composer`        | PoI candidate & block producer   | Strategy, State Machine                |\n| `oracle`          | Off-chain randomness / VRF       | Strategy                               |\n| `monitor`         | Telemetry & health               | Observer                               |\n\n---\n\n## 3. Inter-Service Communication\n\n### 3.1 gRPC Interface Example (Tonic)\n\n```rust\n// crates/minting/src/api.rs\nuse tonic::{Request, Response, Status};\nuse uuid::Uuid;\n\ntonic::include_proto!(\"canvaschain.minting\");\n\n#[derive(Debug, Default)]\npub struct MintingApi;\n\n#[tonic::async_trait]\nimpl minting_server::Minting for MintingApi {\n    async fn compose_nft(\n        &self,\n        req: Request<ComposeRequest>,\n    ) -> Result<Response<ComposeReply>, Status> {\n        let payload = req.into_inner();\n        let nft_id = Uuid::new_v4();\n\n        // Business logic delegates to domain layer.\n        domain::compose_nft(&payload)\n            .await\n            .map_err(|e| Status::internal(e.to_string()))?;\n\n        Ok(Response::new(ComposeReply { nft_id: nft_id.to_string() }))\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 3.2 Event Bus Contract\n\nAll domain events are versioned and serialized with **Prost** + **Serde**.  \nSubject naming convention: `canvas.<service>.<aggregate>.<event>`.\n\n```rust\n// events/src/lib.rs\nuse chrono::{DateTime, Utc};\nuse serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n\npub const SCHEMA_VERSION: &str = \"v1\";\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n#[serde(tag = \"type\", content = \"payload\")]\npub enum Event {\n    NftComposed(NftComposed),\n    BidPlaced(BidPlaced),\n    GovernanceVoteCast(VoteCast),\n    // ...\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, Serialize, Deserialize)]\npub struct NftComposed {\n    pub nft_id: String,\n    pub composer: String,\n    pub timestamp: DateTime<Utc>,\n    pub layers: Vec<String>,\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 4. Core Design Pattern Implementations\n\n### 4.1 Factory Pattern – NFT Instrument Factory\n\n```rust\n// crates/minting/src/factory.rs\nuse super::{error::FactoryError, instruments::*};\n\npub trait InstrumentFactory: Send + Sync {\n    fn spawn(&self, spec: InstrumentSpec) -> Result<Box<dyn Instrument>, FactoryError>;\n}\n\npub struct DefaultFactory;\n\nimpl InstrumentFactory for DefaultFactory {\n    fn spawn(&self, spec: InstrumentSpec) -> Result<Box<dyn Instrument>, FactoryError> {\n        match spec.kind.as_str() {\n            \"Audio\" => Ok(Box::new(AudioInstrument::new(spec)?)),\n            \"Visual\" => Ok(Box::new(VisualInstrument::new(spec)?)),\n            \"Haptic\" => Ok(Box::new(HapticInstrument::new(spec)?)),\n            _ => Err(FactoryError::UnsupportedKind(spec.kind)),\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4.2 Proxy Pattern – Upgradable Smart Contract Proxy\n\n```rust\n// smart_contracts/proxy/src/lib.rs\nuse ink::env::call::{build_call, utils::ReturnType};\nuse ink::prelude::vec::Vec;\nuse ink::primitives::Clear;\n\n#[ink::contract]\nmod proxy {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[ink(storage)]\n    pub struct Proxy {\n        implementation: AccountId,\n        admin: AccountId,\n    }\n\n    impl Proxy {\n        #[ink(constructor)]\n        pub fn new(implementation: AccountId) -> Self {\n self.admin);\n            self.implementation = new_impl;\n        }\n\n        #[ink(message, payable, selector = _)]\n        pub fn fallback(&self) -> Vec<u8> {\n            let result = build_call::<ink::env::DefaultEnvironment>()\n                .call(self.implementation)\n                .transferred_value(Self::env().transferred_value())\n                .exec_input(ink::env::call::ExecutionInput::new(Self::env().selector()).push_arg(Self::env().input()))\n                .returns::<ReturnType<Vec<u8>>>()\n                .fire()\n                .expect(\"proxy call failed\");\n            result\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4.3 Observer Pattern – Trait Evolution Listener\n\n```rust\n// crates/remix/src/observer.rs\nuse serde_json::from_slice::<events::Event>(&msg.payload) {\n            Ok(events::Event::NftComposed(event)) => {\n                debug!(\"Handling NftComposed: {:?}\", event);\n                // State Machine – NFT Lifecycle\n\n```rust\n// domain/src/state_machine.rs\nuse crate::error::StateError;\nuse strum::{Display, EnumIter, EnumString};\n\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, EnumIter, EnumString, Display, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum NftState {\n    Draft,\n    Minted,\n    Listed,\n    Locked,\n    Transferred,\n}\n\nimpl NftState {\n    pub fn transit(self, event: &str) -> Result<NftState, StateError> {\n        use NftState::*;\n        Ok(match (self, event) {\n            (Draft, \"mint\") => Minted,\n            (Minted, \"list\") => Listed,\n            (Listed, \"lock\") => Locked,\n            (Locked, \"transfer\") => Transferred,\n            _ => return Err(StateError::InvalidTransition),\n        })\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### 4.5 Strategy Pattern – Pluggable Cryptographic Curves\n\n```rust\n// crates/crypto/src/strategy.rs\nuse ed25519_dalek as ed25519;\nuse bls_signatures::{PrivateKey as BlsPriv, Serialize as _};\nuse pqcrypto_dilithium::dilithium2;\n\npub trait SigningStrategy: Send + Sync {\n    fn sign(&self, msg: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8>;\n    fn verify(&self, msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> bool;\n}\n\npub struct Ed25519Strategy(ed25519::Keypair);\npub struct BlsStrategy(BlsPriv);\npub struct DilithiumStrategy(dilithium2::keypair::PublicKey, dilithium2::keypair::SecretKey);\n\nimpl SigningStrategy for Ed25519Strategy {\n    fn sign(&self, msg: &[u8]) -> Vec<u8> {\n        self.0.sign(msg).to_bytes().to_vec()\n    }\n    fn verify(&self, msg: &[u8], sig: &[u8]) -> bool {\n        ed25519::Signature::from_bytes(sig)\n            .and_then(|s| self.0.verify(msg, &s).map(|_| ()))\n            .is_ok()\n    }\n}\n\n// Implementations for BlsStrategy and DilithiumStrategy omitted for brevity.\n```\n\n---\n\n## 5. Consensus – Proof-of-Inspiration (PoI)\n\n```rust\n// consensus/src/poi.rs\nuse rand_core::OsRng;\nuse vrf::{openssl::CipherSuite, VRF};\n\npub struct Candidate {\n    pub node_id: String,\n    pub stake: u128,\n    pub curator_score: u64,\n}\n\npub fn elect_composer(candidates: &[Candidate], epoch: u64) -> Option<String> {\n    let total_weight: f64 = candidates\n        .iter()\n        .map(|c| (c.stake as f64) * 0.8 + (c.curator_score as f64) * 0.2)\n        .sum();\n\n    let vrf_seed = derive_epoch_seed(epoch);\n    let mut highest = None;\n    let mut highest_score = 0f64;\n\n    for c in candidates {\n        // VRF output ∈ [0,1)\n        let vrf_out = vrf_draw(&c.node_id, vrf_seed);\n        let weighted = vrf_out * ((c.stake as f64) + 1.0).ln();\n\n        let final_score = weighted / total_weight;\n        if final_score > highest_score {\n            highest_score = final_score;\n            highest = Some(c.node_id.clone());\n        }\n    }\n    highest\n}\n\nfn vrf_draw(node_id: &str, seed: [u8; 32]) -> f64 {\n    let mut csprng = OsRng;\n    let key = CipherSuite::generate_keypair(&mut csprng);\n    let vrf = key\n        .0\n        .prove(&seed)\n        .expect(\"VRF prove failed\")\n        .hash::<sha2::Sha256>();\n    let num = u128::from_le_bytes(vrf[0..16].try_into().unwrap());\n    (num as f64) / (u128::MAX as f64)\n}\n\nfn derive_epoch_seed(epoch: u64) -> [u8; 32] {\n    use sha2::{Digest, Sha256};\n    let mut hasher = Sha256::new();\n    hasher.update(epoch.to_le_bytes());\n    hasher.finalize().into()\n}\n```\n\n---\n\n## 6. Error Handling & Observability\n\n* `thiserror` for domain-level error enums.  \n* `anyhow` at service boundaries for rich context.  \n* `tracing` with OpenTelemetry exporters for logs, metrics and spans.  \n* Circuit-breaker middleware on all gRPC clients using `tower::limit::ConcurrencyLimitFuture`.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Security Considerations\n\n1. Hardware-backed key storage (`yubihsm` feature flag).  \n2. Merkle-proof inclusion for off-chain metadata.  \n3. Re-entrancy guards on Ink! contracts via `non_reentrant` macro.  \n4. Formal verification pipeline (K-framework specs in `/formal_specs`).\n\n---\n\n## 8. Deployment Topology (Mermaid)\n\n```mermaid\ngraph TD\n  A[Users / DApps] -- gRPC --> B[Ingress (Envoy)]\n  B -- gRPC --> C[Wallet Svc]\n  B -- gRPC --> D[Marketplace Svc]\n  B -- gRPC --> E[Governance Svc]\n  subgraph Stateful\n    F[PostgreSQL]\n    G[Pinot Analytics]\n  end\n  C -- SQL --> F\n  D -- SQL --> F\n  E -- SQL --> F\n  D -- Stream --> G\n  subgraph Chain\n    H[(Composer Nodes)]\n  end\n  H -- PoI blocks --> D\n```\n\n---\n\n## 9. Quick-Start Commands\n\n```bash\n# Build all micro-services\ncargo make build-all\n\n# Run a local single-node testnet\ncargo run -p composer -- --dev --in-memory\n```\n\n---\n\n*Document generated: 2024-06-01*\n```","numLines":383,"startLine":1,"totalLines":383}}}

Move all Rust microservices, shared crates (`*-primitives`, `*-proto`, etc.) and infrastructure scripts into a single repository named `canvaschain-symphony`.\n    26→2. Organize code using Cargo workspaces:\n    27→\n    28→```\n    29→canvaschain-symphony/\n    30→├── Cargo.toml            # root workspace manifest\n    31→├── crates/\n    32→│   ├── primitives/\n    33→│   ├── proto/\n    34→│   ├── curve-strategy/\n    35→│   └── …\n    36→└── services/\n    37→    ├── composition/\n    38→    ├── minting/\n    39→    └── …\n    40→```\n    41→\n    42→3. Keep **one** `main` branch protected by mandatory checks. Feature work occurs in PRs with review from at least one other domain owner.\n    43→4. Maintain **semantic versioning per crate** using `cargo-release`. A change to `canvaschain-primitives` bumps only that crate, but CI blocks merging unless all dependants still compile.\n    44→5. Employ a top-level `Makefile.toml` (cargo-make) for common developer tasks (`make db`, `make dev-up`, etc.).\n    45→6. Leverage GitHub Actions’ shared cache: incremental build artifacts (~650 MB) are reused across microservices, cutting CI by ~55 %.\n    46→7. Use code-owners to preserve microservice boundaries:\n    47→\n    48→```\n    49→# CODEOWNERS\n    50→/services/composition/ @art-algo @orchestrator\n    51→/services/marketplace/ @defi-team\n    52→/crates/primitives/ @core-protocol\n    53→```\n    54→\n    55→## Consequences\n    56→\n    57→### Positive\n    58→\n    59→* **Atomic protocol upgrades** across services—e.g., adding a new VRF proof to `ProofOfInspiration` requires one PR, one commit hash.\n    60→* Unified **tooling and linting** (`cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy`, `cargo udeps`, `cargo deny`).\n    61→* **Consistent release pipeline**: `cargo make release` builds Docker images for all changed services with version tags derived from Git metadata.\n    62→* **Simplified local dev**: `make dev-up` spins up all services via `docker-compose`, seeded with testnet state.\n    63→* Easier **refactoring**: `cargo fix --workspace` can propagate changes instantly.\n    64→\n    65→### Negative\n    66→\n    67→* Repo size grows quickly (projected 2 GB in 18 months). We will enforce **Git LFS** for large fixtures (e.g., generative art sample sets).\n    68→* Potential for **cross-team merge conflicts**; mitigated by conventional commits and smaller PR scope.\n    69→* Accidental **hidden coupling**; owners must respect microservice boundaries and avoid reaching into sibling domains.\n    70→\n    71→### Mitigations\n    72→\n    73→* **Bazel remote cache** is on our roadmap for even faster incremental builds.\n    74→* Introduce `cargo hack` to run feature-matrix tests only on changed crates.\n    75→* Quarterly **module sociability reviews** to detect leakage in service boundaries.\n    76→\n    77→---\n    78→\n    79→## Cargo Workspace Manifest (excerpt)\n    80→\n    81→```toml\n    82→[workspace]\n    83→members = [\n    84→    \"crates/primitives\",\n    85→    \"crates/proto\",\n    86→    \"crates/curve-strategy\",\n    87→    \"services/composition\",\n    88→    \"services/minting\",\n    89→    \"services/remixing\",\n    90→    \"services/marketplace\",\n    91→    \"services/royalty\",\n    92→    \"services/wallet\",\n    93→    \"services/governance\",\n    94→    \"services/token-management\",\n    95→    \"services/defi\",\n    96→    \"services/orchestrator\",\n    97→]\n    98→\n    99→[workspace.dependencies]\n   100→tokio          = { version = \"1.37\", features = [\"rt-multi-thread\", \"macros\"] }\n   101→prost          = \"0.12\"\n   102→tonic          = { version = \"0.11\", features = [\"transport\"] }\n   103→tracing        = \"0.1\"\n   104→anyhow         = \"1\"\n   105→thiserror      = \"1\"\n   106→serde          = { version = \"1\", features = [\"derive\"] }\n   107→async-trait    = \"0.1\"\n   108→```\n   109→\n   110→---\n   111→\n   112→## Adoption Plan\n   113→\n   114→1. Freeze external repos; merge them into the new `canvaschain-symphony` monorepo preserving commit history (`git subtree add`).\n   115→2. Update CI to run workspace-aware commands (`cargo test --workspace`).\n   116→3. Notify the community via Discord, update contributor docs, schedule “dev-env day” to migrate local clones.\n   117→4. Archive old repositories as read-only.\n   118→\n   119→---\n   120→\n   121→## References\n   122→\n   123→* RFC-0396 – “Cargo Workspaces in Large Rust Projects”  \n   124→* ADR-004 – “Event Bus over gRPC”  \n   125→* Google SRE Book, Chap. 5 – “Monorepos at Scale”\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. Keep **one** `main` branch protected by mandatory checks. Feature work occurs in PRs with review from at least one other domain owner.\n4. Maintain **semantic versioning per crate** using `cargo-release`. A change to `canvaschain-primitives` bumps only that crate, but CI blocks merging unless all dependants still compile.\n5. Employ a top-level `Makefile.toml` (cargo-make) for common developer tasks (`make db`, `make dev-up`, etc.).\n6. Leverage GitHub Actions’ shared cache: incremental build artifacts (~650 MB) are reused across microservices, cutting CI by ~55 %.\n7. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017ExqeZUCnUUwjxDzi67qgd","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# ADR 002: gRPC for Service-to-Service RPC and NATS Event Bus for Asynchronous Messaging\n     2→\n     3→Date: 2023-11-12  \n     4→Status: Accepted  \n     5→Supersedes: —  \n     6→Superseded by: —  \n     7→Authors: Core Architecture Team (CanvasChain Symphony)\n     8→\n     9→---\n    10→\n    11→## 1. Context\n    12→\n    13→CanvasChain Symphony is composed of ten autonomous Rust micro-services—`composer`, `minter`, `remixer`, `market-maker`, `royalty-streamer`, etc. Each service is owned and deployed independently yet must inter-operate to deliver end-to-end user flows. Two communication patterns are required:\n    14→\n    15→1. **Request / Response (RPC)**  \n    16→   Example: the `market-maker` service must synchronously query `token-registry` for the current royalty split before quoting a swap.\n    17→\n    18→2. **Publish / Subscribe (Eventing)**  \n    19→   Example: the `governance` service votes on a “movement” that triggers the `composer` to mint the next artwork. Every interested service (`gallery-renderer`, `royalty-streamer`, `analytics`) must react to the same chain event without tight coupling.\n    20→\n    21→Key non-functional drivers:\n    22→\n    23→* **Performance:** high throughput, low latency, binary payloads (art layers can be large).\n    24→* **Language Inter-op:** we anticipate plug-ins in Python (ML models), WASM (browser previews), and potentially Solidity-compiled bindings.\n    25→* **Back-Pressure & Delivery Guarantees:** at-least-once delivery for critical NFT state transitions, but fire-and-forget for analytics streams.\n    26→* **Security:** end-to-end encryption (mTLS), pluggable authN/authZ compatible with our wallet integration.\n    27→\n    28→---\n    29→\n    30→## 2. Decision\n    31→\n    32→1. **gRPC** will be adopted for all synchronous, intra-cluster RPC calls.  \n    33→2. **NATS JetStream** will be adopted as the event bus for asynchronous, pub/sub communication.\n    34→\n    35→---\n    36→\n    37→## 3. Rationale\n    38→\n    39→### Why gRPC?\n    40→\n    41→* **IDL-First Development.** Protocol Buffers give us a single source of truth for message schemas in Rust, Python, TypeScript, etc.  \n    42→* **Streaming Support.** Bidirectional streams map naturally to live trait evolution and real-time auction bidding.  \n    43→* **Excellent Rust Ecosystem.** `tonic` provides async/await support, tower middlewares, and first-class mTLS.  \n    44→* **Efficient Binary Encoding.** Artwork layers are sent as deltas or binary blobs; Protobuf minimizes overhead compared to JSON.  \n    45→\n    46→### Why NATS JetStream?\n    47→\n    48→* **Lightweight & Cloud-Native.** Simple single-binary, horizontal-scaling suitability for micro-k8s clusters.  \n    49→* **Exactly-Once Semantics (Opt-In).** JetStream can persist critical events (e.g., ownership transfers) while still allowing fire-and-forget subjects for telemetry.  \n    50→* **Observable.** Built-in tracing, ad-hocs, and integration with Grafana Promtail for distributed tracing.  \n    51→* **Secure Subjects.** Supports leaf-nodes and account isolation, enabling multi-tenant “artist pools.”  \n    52→* **Rust Client Quality.** `nats.rs` offers async, typed-structured messages and JetStream KV for on-chain state mirroring.\n    53→\n    54→### Alternatives Considered\n    55→\n    56→| Option                    | Pros                                              | Cons                                                      |\n    57→|---------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|\n    58→| REST/JSON over HTTP/2     | Ubiquitous, easy to debug                         | Verbose payloads, no streaming, manual schema evolution   |\n    59→| GraphQL Federation        | Flexible querying                                 | Overkill for service-to-service calls, N + 1 traps        |\n    60→| Apache Kafka              | Durable, massive throughput                       | Operationally heavy, zookeeper (or KRaft) complexity      |\n    61→| RabbitMQ                  | Mature, routing patterns                          | Lower raw throughput, heavier client libs for WASM        |\n    62→| ZeroMQ                    | Embedded-style speed                              | No central broker, discovery challenges                   |\n    63→\n    64→gRPC + NATS best satisfied the combined performance, ergonomics, and operational simplicity we need.\n    65→\n    66→---\n    67→\n    68→## 4. Detailed Design\n    69→\n    70→### 4.1 gRPC Service Definitions\n    71→\n    72→Service contracts live in `proto/` and are versioned via Git tags. An excerpt:\n    73→\n    74→```protobuf\n    75→syntax = \"proto3\";\n    76→\n    77→package canvaschain.market.v1;\n    78→\n    79→service RoyaltyService {\n    80→  // Returns the royalty split for a given NFT\n    81→  rpc GetRoyaltyInfo(GetRoyaltyInfoRequest) returns (GetRoyaltyInfoResponse) {\n    82→    option (google.api.http) = {\n    83→      get: \"/v1/royalty/{token_id}\"\n    84→    };\n    85→  }\n    86→}\n    87→\n    88→message GetRoyaltyInfoRequest {\n    89→  uint64 token_id = 1;\n    90→}\n    91→\n    92→message GetRoyaltyInfoResponse {\n    93→  repeated Split split = 1;\n    94→}\n    95→\n    96→message Split {\n    97→  string recipient = 1;         // wallet address\n    98→  uint32 share_bps   = 2;       // basis points\n    99→}\n   100→```\n   101→\n   102→Rust code is generated with `prost` + `tonic_build`:\n   103→\n   104→```rust\n   105→// build.rs\n   106→fn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n   107→    tonic_build::configure()\n   108→        .build_server(true)\n   109→        .build_client(true)\n   110→        .out_dir(\"src/proto\")\n   111→        .compile(&[\n   112→            \"proto/canvaschain/market/v1/royalty_service.proto\",\n   113→        ], &[\"proto\"])?;\n   114→    Ok(())\n   115→}\n   116→```\n   117→\n   118→### 4.2 gRPC Infrastructure Patterns\n   119→\n   120→* **Interceptors:** Uniform wallet-signature validation (`x-canvas-sig`), request tracing (OpenTelemetry).  \n   121→* **Resilience:** `tower::retry::Retry` with exponential backoff for transient failures.  \n   122→* **Codegen Regeneration:** GitHub Action enforces that `cargo build --workspace` fails if `proto/` drift is detected.\n   123→\n   124→### 4.3 NATS JetStream Topology\n   125→\n state changes, storage TTL = 365 d, ack = explicit  \n   131→  * `telemetry.metrics` – non-critical stats, storage TTL = 1 d, ack = none  \n   132→* **Consumers:**  \n   133→  * Durable pull consumer per microservice (`composer_core`, `gallery_renderer`, …).  \n   134→  * Ephemeral consumers for CLI tools and data scientists.\n   135→\n   136→Rust subscription example:\n   137→\n   138→```rust\n   139→let event: MintedEvent = jetstream\n   140→    .get_stream(\"core.events\")?\n   141→    .create_consumer(ConsumerConfig {\n   142→        durable_name: Some(\"gallery_renderer\".to_string()),\n   143→        filter_subject: \"nft.composer.v1.artwork.minted\".into(),\n   144→        deliver_policy: DeliverPolicy::All,\n   145→        ack_policy: AckPolicy::Explicit,\n   146→        ..Default::default()\n   147→    })?\n   148→    .messages()?\n   149→    .next()\n   150→    .await?\n   151→    .unwrap()\n   152→    .deserialize()?;\n   153→```\n   154→\n   155→### 4.4 Transactional Outbox Pattern\n   156→\n   157→To avoid dual-write hazards between the PostgreSQL command side and the event bus, each service implements the **Transactional Outbox** pattern. A reliable background task (`tokio::spawn`) pulls unsent events, publishes to NATS, and marks them dispatched. This guarantees at-least-once delivery even if the service crashes mid-transaction.\n   158→\n   159→![Transactional Outbox Diagram](../../design/diagrams/transactional_outbox.png)\n   160→\n   161→---\n   162→\n   163→## 5. Security Considerations\n   164→\n   165→* **mTLS Everywhere:** Shared SPIFFE IDs (`spiffe://canvaschain/{service}`) issued by cert-manager.  \n   166→* **Subject ACLs:** Only the `composer` may publish on `nft.composer.*`; others read-only.  \n   167→* **JWT Claims:** gRPC headers leverage short-lived JWTs signed by the `auth` service, propagated via NATS headers for traceability.  \n   168→* **Replay Protection:** Nonces + timestamp fields in all state-changing Protobuf messages.\n   169→\n   170→---\n   171→\n   172→## 6. Consequences\n   173→\n   174→### Positive\n   175→\n   176→* Consistent developer experience across sync and async messaging.\n   177→* Performance-optimized binary encoding keeps latency < 15 ms P99 inside the same AZ.\n   178→* Pluggable structure: any service can be rewritten in another language with minimal friction.\n   179→* Observability is unified via OpenTelemetry exporters for `tonic` and `nats.rs`.\n   180→\n   181→### Negative / Trade-offs\n   182→\n   183→* Learning curve for contributors unfamiliar with Protocol Buffers.\n   184→* Operationally, we must maintain a NATS cluster (RAFT group) in addition to the Postgres DB.\n   185→* Protobuf evolution requires backward-compatibility discipline to avoid breaking older miners.\n   186→\n   187→---\n   188→\n   189→## 7. Measures of Success\n   190→\n   191→1. End-to-end mint-to-marketplace latency ≤ 200 ms on staging.\n   192→2. Zero lost critical events during chaos-testing (`chaos-nats` network partitions).\n   193→3. Successful inter-op PoC of Python `remixer` plugin subscribing to `nft.*` events.\n   194→\n   195→---\n   196→\n   197→## 8. References\n   198→\n   199→* NATS JetStream Design: https://docs.nats.io/jetstream  \n   200→* tonic gRPC Rust Library: https://github.com/hyperium/tonic  \n   201→* “Transactional Outbox” by Pat Helland: https://microservices.io/patterns/data/transactional-outbox.html  \n   202→* gRPC Security Best Practices: https://grpc.io/docs/guides/security/\n   203→\n   204→---\n   205→\n   206→*(END OF ADR)*\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"c7d69bdc-7736-4756-961f-b1f93b4bd16f","uuid":"012cbf6d-457e-4b45-b71f-3e3175460f22","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"/app/project/docs/adr/002-grpc-and-event-bus.md","content":"# ADR 002: gRPC for Service-to-Service RPC and NATS Event Bus for Asynchronous Messaging\n\nDate: 2023-11-12  \nStatus: Accepted  \nSupersedes: —  \nSuperseded by: —  \nAuthors: Core Architecture Team (CanvasChain Symphony)\n\n---\n\n## 1. Context\n\nCanvasChain Symphony is composed of ten autonomous Rust micro-services—`composer`, `minter`, `remixer`, `market-maker`, `royalty-streamer`, etc. Each service is owned and deployed independently yet must inter-operate to deliver end-to-end user flows. Two communication patterns are required:\n\n1. **Request / Response (RPC)**  \n   Example: the `market-maker` service must synchronously query `token-registry` for the current royalty split before quoting a swap.\n\n2. **Publish / Subscribe (Eventing)**  \n   Example: the `governance` service votes on a “movement” that triggers the `composer` to mint the next artwork. Every interested service (`gallery-renderer`, `royalty-streamer`, `analytics`) must react to the same chain event without tight coupling.\n\nKey non-functional drivers:\n\n* **Performance:** high throughput, low latency, binary payloads (art layers can be large).\n* **Language Inter-op:** we anticipate plug-ins in Python (ML models), WASM (browser previews), and potentially Solidity-compiled bindings.\n* **Back-Pressure & Delivery Guarantees:** at-least-once delivery for critical NFT state transitions, but fire-and-forget for analytics streams.\n* **Security:** end-to-end encryption (mTLS), pluggable authN/authZ compatible with our wallet integration.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Decision\n\n1. **gRPC** will be adopted for all synchronous, intra-cluster RPC calls.  \n2. **NATS JetStream** will be adopted as the event bus for asynchronous, pub/sub communication.\n\n---\n\n## 3. Rationale\n\n### Why gRPC?\n\n* **IDL-First Development.** Protocol Buffers give us a single source of truth for message schemas in Rust, Python, TypeScript, etc.  \n* **Streaming Support.** Bidirectional streams map naturally to live trait evolution and real-time auction bidding.  \n* **Excellent Rust Ecosystem.** `tonic` provides async/await support, tower middlewares, and first-class mTLS.  \n* **Efficient Binary Encoding.** Artwork layers are sent as deltas or binary blobs; Protobuf minimizes overhead compared to JSON.  \n\n### Why NATS JetStream?\n\n* **Lightweight & Cloud-Native.** Simple single-binary, horizontal-scaling suitability for micro-k8s clusters.  \n* **Exactly-Once Semantics (Opt-In).** JetStream can persist critical events (e.g., ownership transfers) while still allowing fire-and-forget subjects for telemetry.  \n* **Observable.** Built-in tracing, ad-hocs, and integration with Grafana Promtail for distributed tracing.  \n* **Secure Subjects.** Supports leaf-nodes and account isolation, enabling multi-tenant “artist pools.”  \n* **Rust Client Quality.** `nats.rs` offers async, typed-structured messages and JetStream KV for on-chain state mirroring.\n\n### Alternatives Considered\n\n| Option                    | Pros                                              | Cons                                                      |\n|---------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|-----------------------------------------------------------|\n| REST/JSON over HTTP/2     | Ubiquitous, easy to debug                         | Verbose payloads, no streaming, manual schema evolution   |\n| GraphQL Federation        | Flexible querying                                 | Overkill for service-to-service calls, N + 1 traps        |\n| Apache Kafka              | Durable, massive throughput                       | Operationally heavy, zookeeper (or KRaft) complexity      |\n| RabbitMQ                  | Mature, routing patterns                          | Lower raw throughput, heavier client libs for WASM        |\n| ZeroMQ                    | Embedded-style speed                              | No central broker, discovery challenges                   |\n\ngRPC + NATS best satisfied the combined performance, ergonomics, and operational simplicity we need.\n\n---\n\n## 4. Detailed Design\n\n### 4.1 gRPC Service Definitions\n\nService contracts live in `proto/` and are versioned via Git tags. An excerpt:\n\n```protobuf\nsyntax = \"proto3\";\n\npackage canvaschain.market.v1;\n\nservice RoyaltyService {\n  // Returns the royalty split for a given NFT\n  rpc GetRoyaltyInfo(GetRoyaltyInfoRequest) returns (GetRoyaltyInfoResponse) {\n    option (google.api.http) = {\n      get: \"/v1/royalty/{token_id}\"\n    };\n  }\n}\n\nmessage GetRoyaltyInfoRequest {\n  uint64 token_id = 1;\n}\n\nmessage GetRoyaltyInfoResponse {\n  repeated Split split = 1;\n}\n\nmessage Split {\n  string recipient = 1;         // wallet address\n  uint32 share_bps   = 2;       // basis points\n}\n```\n\nRust code is generated with `prost` + `tonic_build`:\n\n```rust\n// build.rs\nfn main() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    tonic_build::configure()\n        .build_server(true)\n        .build_client(true)\n        .out_dir(\"src/proto\")\n        .compile(&[\n            \"proto/canvaschain/market/v1/royalty_service.proto\",\n        ], &[\"proto\"])?;\n    Ok(())\n}\n```\n\n### 4.2 gRPC Infrastructure Patterns\n\n* **Interceptors:** Uniform wallet-signature validation (`x-canvas-sig`), request tracing (OpenTelemetry).  \n* **Resilience:** `tower::retry::Retry` with exponential backoff for transient failures.  \n* **Codegen Regeneration:** GitHub Action enforces that `cargo build --workspace` fails if `proto/` drift is detected.\n\n### 4.3 NATS JetStream Topology\n\nSubject naming convention: `domain.context.version.resource.event`  \nExample: `nft.composer.v1.artwork.minted`\n\n* **Streams:**  \n  * `core.events` – all critical state changes, storage TTL = 365 d, ack = explicit  \n  * `telemetry.metrics` – non-critical stats, storage TTL = 1 d, ack = none  \n* **Consumers:**  \n  * Durable pull consumer per microservice (`composer_core`, `gallery_renderer`, …).  \n  * Ephemeral consumers for CLI tools and data scientists.\n\nRust subscription example:\n\n```rust\nlet event: MintedEvent = jetstream\n    .get_stream(\"core.events\")?\n    .create_consumer(ConsumerConfig {\n        durable_name: Some(\"gallery_renderer\".to_string()),\n        filter_subject: \"nft.composer.v1.artwork.minted\".into(),\n        deliver_policy: DeliverPolicy::All,\n        ack_policy: AckPolicy::Explicit,\n        ..Default::default()\n    })?\n    .messages()?\n    .next()\n    .await?\n    .unwrap()\n    .deserialize()?;\n```\n\n### 4.4 Transactional Outbox Pattern\n\nTo avoid dual-write hazards between the PostgreSQL command side and the event bus, each service implements the **Transactional Outbox** pattern. A reliable background task (`tokio::spawn`) pulls unsent events, publishes to NATS, and marks them dispatched. This guarantees at-least-once delivery even if the service crashes mid-transaction.\n\n![Transactional Outbox Diagram](../../design/diagrams/transactional_outbox.png)\n\n---\n\n## 5. Security Considerations\n\n* **mTLS Everywhere:** Shared SPIFFE IDs (`spiffe://canvaschain/{service}`) issued by cert-manager.  \n* **Subject ACLs:** Only the `composer` may publish on `nft.composer.*`; others read-only.  \n* **JWT Claims:** gRPC headers leverage short-lived JWTs signed by the `auth` service, propagated via NATS headers for traceability.  \n* **Replay Protection:** Nonces + timestamp fields in all state-changing Protobuf messages.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Consequences\n\n### Positive\n\n* Consistent developer experience across sync and async messaging.\n* Performance-optimized binary encoding keeps latency < 15 ms P99 inside the same AZ.\n* Pluggable structure: any service can be rewritten in another language with minimal friction.\n* Observability is unified via OpenTelemetry exporters for `tonic` and `nats.rs`.\n\n### Negative / Trade-offs\n\n* Learning curve for contributors unfamiliar with Protocol Buffers.\n* Operationally, we must maintain a NATS cluster (RAFT group) in addition to the Postgres DB.\n* Protobuf evolution requires backward-compatibility discipline to avoid breaking older miners.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Measures of Success\n\n1. End-to-end mint-to-marketplace latency ≤ 200 ms on staging.\n2. Zero lost critical events during chaos-testing (`chaos-nats` network partitions).\n3. Successful inter-op PoC of Python `remixer` plugin subscribing to `nft.*` events.\n\n---\n\n## 8. References\n\n* NATS JetStream Design: https://docs.nats.io/jetstream  \n* tonic gRPC Rust Library: https://github.com/hyperium/tonic  \n* “Transactional Outbox” by Pat Helland: https://microservices.io/patterns/data/transactional-outbox.html  \n* gRPC Security Best Practices: https://grpc.io/docs/guides/security/\n\n---\n\n*(END OF ADR)*","numLines":206,"startLine":1,"totalLines":206}}}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Avx6MWLQT4p89qH6m3X46Y","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→```rust\n     2→//! Core NFT domain types and evolution/state-machine logic for the\n     3→//! CanvasChain Symphony platform.\n     4→//!\n     5→//! This module purposefully remains *no_std*-friendly (with an opt-in\n     6→//! `std` feature for clients that need heap allocation) so it can be\n     7→//! reused inside on-chain WASM smart contracts as well as off-chain\n     8→//! micro-services.\n     9→//!\n    10→//! Key design goals:\n    11→//! 1. Multi-layer NFTs with independent ownership & royalty streams.\n    12→//! 2. Fine-grained state transitions driven by user interaction,\n    13→//!    governance votes, staking incentives and DeFi metrics.\n    14→//! 3. Pluggable cryptography via Strategy pattern (`SignatureScheme`).\n    15→//!\n    16→//! self.normalize_royalties()?;\n   355→        // Freeze if stake duration > 30 days.\n   356→        if duration.as_secs() >= 30 * 24 * 3600 {\n   357→            for layer in self.layers.values_mut() {\n   358→                layer.freeze()?;\n   359→            }\n   360→        }\n   361→        Ok(())\n   362→    }\n   363→\n   364→    fn handle_governance(\n   365→        &mut self,\n   366→        approve_ratio: BasisPoints,\n   367→    ) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n   368→        if approve_ratio.as_u16() >= self.governance.super_majority_bp.as_u16()\n   369→        {\n   370→ schedule. .values()\n            .map(|bp| bp.as_u16() as u32)\n            .sum();\n        if total > BasisPoints::MAX as u32 {\n            return Err(NftError::RoyaltyOverflow);\n        }\n        Ok(Self { recipients })\n    }\n\n    /// Resolve the royalty amount for each recipient given a sale price.\n    pub fn split(\n        &self,\n        sale_price: TokenAmount,\n    ) -> BTreeMap<WalletAddress, TokenAmount> {\n        self.recipients\n            .iter()\n            .map(|(addr, bp)| {\n                let amount = sale_price * bp.as_u16() as u128\n                    / BasisPoints::MAX as u128;\n                (addr.clone(), amount)\n            })\n            .collect()\n    }\n}\n\n/// Governance parameters per NFT.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, Serialize, /// is preferable, but we hash a concatenation for simplicity).\n    pub fn digest(&self) -> blake3::Hash {\n        let mut hasher = blake3::Hasher::new();\n        for layer in self.layers.values() {\n            hasher.update(layer.cid.as_bytes());\n        }\n        hasher.finalize()\n    }\n\n    /// Attempt to mutate the NFT based on an external event.\n    pub fn evolve(\n        &mut self,\n        event: EvolutionEvent,\n    ) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n        match event {\n            EvolutionEvent::Interaction { wallet, payload } => {\n                self.handle_interaction(wallet, payload)\n            }\n            EvolutionEvent::Staked {\n                wallet,\n                amount,\n                duration,\n            } => self.handle_stake(wallet, amount, duration),\n            EvolutionEvent::GovernanceResult {\n                proposal_id: _,\n                approve_ratio,\n            } => self.handle_governance(approve_ratio),\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn handle_interaction(\n        &mut self,\n        wallet: WalletAddress,\n        _payload: Vec<u8>,\n    ) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n        // Simple rule: first interaction grants temporary co-ownership\n        // (until frozen) of layer[0] (Visual) if not already owned.\n        if let Some(first_layer) = self.layers.values_mut().next() {\n            if first_layer.state == LayerState::Active\n                && first_layer.owner != wallet\n            {\n                first_layer.owner = wallet;\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    fn handle_stake(\n        &mut self,\n        _wallet: WalletAddress,\n        amount: TokenAmount,\n        duration: Duration,\n    ) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n        // Increase royalty shares proportionally to stake (toy logic).\n        let bonus_bp = (amount / 1_000_000_000_000_000_000u128) as u16; // 1 ETH == 10 bp\n        let bonus_bp = bonus_bp.min(1000); // cap at +10%\n        let adjusted = BasisPoints::new(bonus_bp)?;\n        // Add a dummy recipient (treasury) with the bonus share.\n        self.royalties.recipients.insert(\n            WalletAddress(\"canvas_treasury\".into()),\n            adjusted,\n        );\n        // Re-balance to avoid overflow (leftover goes to creator).\n        self.normalize_royalties()?;\n        // Freeze if stake duration > 30 days.\n        if duration.as_secs() >= 30 * 24 * 3600 {\n            for layer in self.layers.values_mut() {\n                layer.freeze()?;\n            }\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    fn handle_governance(\n        &mut self,\n        approve_ratio: BasisPoints,\n    ) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n        if approve_ratio.as_u16() >= self.governance.super_majority_bp.as_u16()\n        {\n            // Promote all `Draft` layers to `Active`.\n            for layer in self.layers.values_mut() {\n                if layer.state == LayerState::Draft {\n                    layer.state = LayerState::Active;\n                }\n            }\n            Ok(())\n        } else {\n            Err(NftError::Governance(\n                \"proposal rejected\".to_string(),\n            ))\n        }\n    }\n\n    fn normalize_royalties(&mut self) -> Result<(), NftError> {\n        let total: u32 = self\n            .royalties\n            .recipients\n            .values()\n            .map(|bp| bp.as_u16() as u32)\n            .sum();\n        if total > BasisPoints::MAX as u32 {\n            return Err(NftError::RoyaltyOverflow);\n        }\n        Ok(())\n    }\n\n    /// Transfer a specific layer to a new owner.\n    pub fn transfer_layer(\n        &mut self,\n        layer_id: .ok_or(NftError::UnknownLayer(layer_id))?;\n        layer.transfer(from, to)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Fluent builder for `MultiLayerNft`.\npub struct NftBuilder {\n    id: Uuid,\n    name: String,\n    creator: WalletAddress,\n    layers: BTreeMap<Uuid, NftLayer>,\n    governance: Option<GovernancePolicy>,\n    royalties: Option<RoyaltySchedule>,\n}\n\nimpl NftBuilder {\n    fn new(name: String,      royalties: None,\n        }\n    }\n\n    pub fn add_layer(\n        mut self,\n        kind: NftLayerKind,\n        cid: impl Into<String>,\n        initial_state: LayerState,\n    owner: self.creator.clone(),\n            royalty_bp,\n            created_at: Self::now(),\n            last_updated: Self::now(),\n        };\n        self.layers.insert(layer.id, layer);\n royalty_schedule(\n        mut self,\n        schedule: RoyaltySchedule,\n    ) -> Self {\n        self.royalties = Some(schedule);\n        self\n    }\n\n    pub fn build(self) -> Result<MultiLayerNft, NftError> {\n        let governance = self.governance.unwrap_or(GovernancePolicy {\n            quorum_bp: BasisPoints::new(2_000)?,\n            super_majority_bp: BasisPoints::new(6_600)?,\n            voting_period: Duration::from_secs(7 * 24 * 3600),\n        });\n\n        let royalties = self.royalties.unwrap_or_else(|| {\n            let mut map = BTreeMap::new();\n            map.insert(self.creator.clone(), BasisPoints(1_000)); // 10%\n            RoyaltySchedule { recipients: map }\n        });\n\n        Ok(MultiLayerNft {\n            id: self.id,\n            name: self.name,\n            creator: self.creator,\n            layers: self.layers,\n            governance,\n            royalties,\n        })\n    }\n\n    #[inline(always)]\n    fn now() -> u64 {\n        // Use UNIX time in seconds. On chain we replace this with\n        // block-timestamp to avoid dependence on wall clock.\n        #[cfg(feature = \"std\")]\n        {\n            use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n            SystemTime::now()\n                .duration_since(UNIX_EPOCH)\n                .unwrap_or_default()\n                .as_secs()\n        }\n        #[cfg(not(feature = \"std\"))]\n        {\n            0\n        }\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":512,"startLine":1,"totalLines":512}}}

Node service state–machine\n     3→//!\n     4→//! This module implements a hierarchical, asynchronous state-machine that\n     5→//! drives a single CanvasChain Symphony “conductor” (validator / full node).\n     6→//!\n     7→//! The public façade is [`NodeStateMachine::spawn`].  Callers provide a\n     8→//! configured [`NodeContext`] plus a Tokio `mpsc::Receiver<NodeEvent>` that\n     9→//! represents the node’s inbound event bus.  \n    10→//!\n    11→//! The machine is built around the classic State pattern.  Each state\n    12→//! implements [`State`]—an async callback that receives the next event and\n    13→//! returns a [`Transition`] indicating whether the machine should:\n    14→//!   * stay in the same state\n    15→//!   * transition to a new boxed state\n    16→//!   * perform a graceful shutdown.\n    17→//!\n    18→//! Transitions are **total**; all event/state combinations _must_ resolve to a\n    19→//! `Transition`, preventing undefined behaviour at runtime.\n    20→//!\n    21→//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    22→//! Conventions & best-practices\n    23→//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    24→//! • Logging is performed with the `tracing` crate (structured, async-aware).  \n    25→//! • All public types derive `Debug` to make tracing easier.  \n    26→//! • Errors bubble up using `thiserror::Error` and are logged before causing a\n    27→//!   transition into [`StateError`].  \n    28→//! • No blocking I/O—everything is fully async.  \n    29→//! • `#![deny(clippy::unwrap_used)]` ensures we never `.unwrap()` outside of\n    30→//!   tests.  \n    31→//!\n    32→//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    33→//! Example\n    34→//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    35→//! ```no_run\n    36→//! # use tokio::sync::mpsc;\n    37→//! # use canvas_chain_node_service::state_machine::*;\n    38→//! # #[tokio::main]\n    39→//! # async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n    40→//! let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel(128);\n    41→//! let ctx = NodeContext::new(Default::default(), tx.clone());\n    42→//! let handle = NodeStateMachine::spawn(ctx, rx);\n    43→//!\n    44→//! // Send a dummy event\n    45→//! tx.send(NodeEvent::Tick).await?;\n    46→//!\n    47→//! // Wait until the machine exits\n    48→//! handle.await?;\n    49→//! # Ok::<_, anyhow::Error>(())\n    50→//! # }\n    51→//! ```\n    52→\n    53→#![deny(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n    54→\n    55→use std::time::Duration;\n    56→\n    57→use async_trait::async_trait;\n    58→use tokio::{\n    59→    select,\n    60→    sync::mpsc,\n    61→    task::JoinHandle,\n    62→    time::{interval, Interval},\n    63→};\n    64→use tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument};\n    65→\n    66→use crate::event_bus::Publish;\n    67→use crate::network::types::{Block, PeerId};\n    68→use crate::storage::Database;\n    69→\n    70→// ------------------------------\n    71→// Public facade\n    72→// ------------------------------\n    73→\n    74→/// Spawn a state-machine on the Tokio runtime and return a [`JoinHandle`]\n    75→/// representing its lifecycle.\n    76→pub struct NodeStateMachine;\n    77→\n    78→impl NodeStateMachine {\n    79→    /// Spawns the asynchronous state machine. Node service state–machine\n//!\n//! This module implements a hierarchical, asynchronous state-machine that\n//! drives a single CanvasChain Symphony “conductor” (validator / full node).\n//!\n//! The public façade is [`NodeStateMachine::spawn`].  Callers provide a\n//! configured [`NodeContext`] plus a Tokio `mpsc::Receiver<NodeEvent>` that\n//! represents the node’s inbound event bus.  \n//!\n//! The machine is built around the classic State pattern.  Each state\n//! implements [`State`]—an async callback that receives the next event and\n//! returns a [`Transition`] indicating whether the machine should:\n//!   * stay in the same state\n//!   * transition to a new boxed state\n//!   * perform a graceful shutdown.\n//!\n//! Transitions are **total**; all event/state combinations _must_ resolve to a\n//! `Transition`, preventing undefined behaviour at runtime.\n//!\n//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! Conventions & best-practices\n//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! • Logging is performed with the `tracing` crate (structured, async-aware).  \n//! • All public types derive `Debug` to make tracing easier.  \n//! • Errors bubble up using `thiserror::Error` and are logged before causing a\n//!   transition into [`StateError`].  \n//! • No blocking I/O—everything is fully async.  \n//! • `#![deny(clippy::unwrap_used)]` ensures we never `.unwrap()` outside of\n//!   tests.  \n//!\n//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! Example\n//! -------------------------------------------------------------------------\n//! ```no_run\n//! # use tokio::sync::mpsc;\n//! # use canvas_chain_node_service::state_machine::*;\n//! # #[tokio::main]\n//! # async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {\n//! let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel(128);\n//! let ctx = NodeContext::new(Default::default(), tx.clone());\n//! let handle = NodeStateMachine::spawn(ctx, rx);\n//!\n//! // Send a dummy event\n//! tx.send(NodeEvent::Tick).await?;\n//!\n//! // Wait until the machine exits\n//! handle.await?;\n//! # Ok::<_, anyhow::Error>(())\n//! # }\n//! ```\n\n#![deny(clippy::unwrap_used)]\n\nuse std::time::Duration;\n\nuse async_trait::async_trait;\nuse tokio::{\n    select,\n    sync::mpsc,\n    task::JoinHandle,\n    time::{interval, Interval},\n};\nuse tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument};\n\nuse crate::event_bus::Publish;\nuse crate::network::types::{Block, PeerId};\nuse crate::storage::Database;\n\n// ------------------------------\n// Public facade\n// ------------------------------\n\n/// Spawn a state-machine on the Tokio runtime and return a [`JoinHandle`]\n/// representing its lifecycle.\npub struct NodeStateMachine;\n\nimpl NodeStateMachine {\n    /// Spawns the asynchronous state machine.  The function immediately returns\n    /// a Tokio [`JoinHandle`] that finishes when the machine reaches the\n    /// [`Transition::Shutdown`] transition or the provided `event_rx` closes.\n    pub fn spawn(\n        mut ctx: NodeContext,\n        mut event_rx: mpsc::Receiver<NodeEvent>,\n    ) -> JoinHandle<anyhow::Result<()>> {\n        tokio::spawn(async move {\n            let mut state: Box<dyn State> = Box::<StateBooting>::default();\n\n            // Heart-beat ticker; every state receives `Tick` events.\n            let mut ticker = interval(Duration::from_secs(5));\n\n            loop {\n                select! {\n                    maybe_evt = event_rx.recv() => {\n                        match maybe_evt {\n                            Some(evt) => {\n                                state = dispatch(&mut ctx, state, evt).await?;\n                            }\n                            None => {\n                                info!(\"event stream closed – shutting node down\");\n                                break;\n                            }\n                        }\n                    }\n\n                    _ = ticker.tick() => {\n                        state = dispatch(&mut ctx, state, NodeEvent::Tick).await?;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n\n            info!(node_id=%ctx.config.node_id, \"node service terminated\");\n            Ok(())\n        })\n    }\n}\n\n// ------------------------------\n// Context & Events\n// ------------------------------\n\n/// Immutable configuration for a running node.\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct NodeConfig {\n    pub node_id: String,\n    pub network_id: String,\n    pub database_url: String,\n    pub is_validator: bool,\n}\n\nimpl Default for NodeConfig {\n    fn default() -> Self {\n        Self {\n            node_id: nanoid::nanoid!(),\n            network_id: \"canvas-testnet\".into(),\n            database_url: \"sqlite::memory:\".into(),\n            is_validator: false,\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Long-living, shared runtime context passed to every [`State`].\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub struct NodeContext {\n    pub config: NodeConfig,\n    pub db: Database,\n    pub event_tx: mpsc::Sender<NodeEvent>,\n    pub bus: Publish, // Simplified, re-export from crate::event_bus\n}\n\nimpl NodeContext {\n    pub fn new(config: NodeConfig, event_tx: mpsc::Sender<NodeEvent>) -> Self {\n        let db = Database::connect(&config.database_url)\n            .expect(\"infallible for in-memory; otherwise bubble up\");\n\n        Self {\n            config,\n            db,\n            event_tx,\n            bus: Publish::default(),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// All possible events handled by the state machine.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub enum NodeEvent {\n    /// Periodic internal heart-beat.\n    Tick,\n\n    /// New peer connected\n    PeerConnected(PeerId),\n\n    /// Network broadcast of a new block announcement.\n    BlockReceived(Block),\n\n    /// The consensus engine elected this node the next composer (“validator”).\n    ElectedComposer,\n\n    /// Graceful shutdown request.\n    Shutdown,\n}\n\n// ------------------------------\n// State machinery\n// ------------------------------\n\n/// When a [`State`] finishes processing an event it returns a `Transition`\n/// deciding the machine’s next state.\n#[derive(Debug)]\npub enum Transition {\n    /// Remain in the existing state.\n    Stay,\n    /// Replace current state with a new one.\n    Next(Box<dyn State>),\n    /// Terminate the machine.\n    Shutdown,\n}\n\n/// Common interface implemented by *every* node state.\n#[async_trait]\npub trait State: Send {\n    /// Human-readable name of the state (`&'static str` avoids allocations).\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str;\n\n    /// Process an incoming event. Must return a valid [`Transition`].\n    async fn on_event(\n        &mut self,\n        ctx: &mut NodeContext,\n        event: NodeEvent,\n    ) -> anyhow::Result<Transition>;\n}\n\n// Wrapper that logs state entry / exit\n#[instrument(skip_all, fields(new_state = next.name()))]\nasync fn dispatch(\n    ctx: &mut NodeContext,\n    mut current: Box<dyn State>,\n    evt: NodeEvent,\n) -> anyhow::Result<Box<dyn State>> {\n    debug!(\n        state = current.name(),\n        evt = format!(\"{:?}\", evt),\n        \"handling event\"\n    );\n\n    let transition = match current.on_event(ctx, evt).await {\n        Ok(t) => t,\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\"state {:?} returned error: {e:?}\", current.name());\n            return Ok(Box::new(StateError::new(e)));\n        }\n    };\n\n    let next: Box<dyn State> = match transition {\n        Transition::Stay => current,\n        Transition::Next(next_state) => next_state,\n        Transition::Shutdown => {\n            info!(\"shutdown requested by state {}\", current.name());\n            return Err(anyhow::anyhow!(\"state-machine shutdown\"));\n        }\n    };\n\n    Ok(next)\n}\n\n// ------------------------------\n// Concrete States\n// ------------------------------\n\n/// Initial boot / self-check state.\n#[derive(Default)]\nstruct StateBooting;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl State for StateBooting {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"Booting\"\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    async fn on_event(\n        &mut self,\n        ctx: &mut NodeContext,\n        event: NodeEvent,\n    ) -> anyhow::Result<Transition> {\n        match event {\n            NodeEvent::Tick => {\n                info!(\"boot sequence: verifying database connectivity\");\n                ctx.db.healthcheck().await?;\n                info!(\"boot sequence: connecting to peer network\");\n                // Suppose we send some handshake event via ctx.bus here  \n                // ctx.bus.publish(...)\n\n                Ok(Transition::Next(Box::<StateSyncing>::default()))\n            }\n            NodeEvent::Shutdown => Ok(Transition::Shutdown),\n            _ => Ok(Transition::Stay),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Synchronising blockchain data with peers.\n#[derive(Default)]\nstruct StateSyncing;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl State for StateSyncing {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"Syncing\"\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    async fn on_event(\n        &mut self,\n        ctx: &mut NodeContext,\n        event: NodeEvent,\n    ) -> anyhow::Result<Transition> {\n        match event {\n            NodeEvent::BlockReceived(block) => {\n                info!(height = block.header.height, \"received block during sync\");\n                ctx.db.insert_block(block).await?;\n                // For brevity we assume once at tip we move to Active\n                if ctx.db.is_at_chain_tip().await? {\n                    info!(\"local chain at tip – moving to Active state\");\n                    return Ok(Transition::Next(Box::<StateActive>::default()));\n                }\n                Ok(Transition::Stay)\n            }\n            NodeEvent::Shutdown => Ok(Transition::Shutdown),\n            _ => Ok(Transition::Stay),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Fully synced, participating in consensus and answering RPCs.\n#[derive(Default)]\nstruct StateActive;\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl State for StateActive {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"Active\"\n    }\n\n    #[instrument(skip_all)]\n    async fn on_event(\n        &mut self,\n        ctx: &mut NodeContext,\n        event: NodeEvent,\n    ) -> anyhow::Result<Transition> {\n        match event {\n            NodeEvent::ElectedComposer if ctx.config.is_validator => {\n                info!(\"🎵 This node has been elected composer – creating art block\");\n                let block = compose_next_block(ctx).await?;\n                ctx.bus.publish(NodeEvent::BlockReceived(block.clone()))\n                    .await\n                    .map_err(|e| anyhow::anyhow!(\"failed to publish block: {e}\"))?;\n                ctx.db.insert_block(block).await?;\n                Ok(Transition::Stay)\n            }\n            NodeEvent::BlockReceived(block) => {\n                debug!(\n                    height = block.header.height,\n                    composer = %block.header.producer,\n                    \"applying canonical block\"\n                );\n                ctx.db.insert_block(block).await?;\n                Ok(Transition::Stay)\n            }\n            NodeEvent::Shutdown => Ok(Transition::Shutdown),\n            _ => Ok(Transition::Stay),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Terminal error state—any failure bubbles here so that the system can exit\n/// gracefully instead of panicking.\n#[derive(Debug)]\nstruct StateError {\n    cause: anyhow::Error,\n}\n\nimpl StateError {\n    fn new<E: Into<anyhow::Error>>(e: E) -> Self {\n        Self { cause: e.into() }\n    }\n}\n\n#[async_trait]\nimpl State for StateError {\n    fn name(&self) -> &'static str {\n        \"Error\"\n    }\n\n    async fn on_event(\n        &mut self,\n        _ctx: &mut NodeContext,\n        _event: NodeEvent,\n    ) -> anyhow::Result<Transition> {\n        error!(\"node is in error state: {:?}\", self.cause);\n        Ok(Transition::Shutdown)\n    }\n}\n\n// ------------------------------\n// Helper functions\n// ------------------------------\n\n/// Compose a new “art block” as elected composer.\n///\n/// This is merely a stub for the example.  A real implementation would run the\n/// Proof-of-Inspiration algorithm, gather pending transactions from the mem-\n/// pool, merge generative art layers, compute state roots, sign the header,\n/// etc.\nasync fn compose_next_block(ctx: &NodeContext) -> anyhow::Result<Block> {\n    use rand::Rng;\n\n    let mut rng = rand::thread_rng();\n    let block = Block {\n        header: crate::network::types::BlockHeader {\n            height: ctx.db.latest_height().await? + 1,\n            producer: ctx.config.node_id.clone(),\n            timestamp: chrono::Utc::now(),\n            nonce: rng.gen::<u64>(),\n            prev_hash: ctx.db.latest_hash().await?,\n            merkle_root: vec![0; 32], // placeholder\n            signature: vec![],\n        },\n        transactions: vec![],\n    };\n    Ok(block)\n}\n\n// ------------------------------\n// Tests\n// ------------------------------\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n    use tokio::time::timeout;\n\n    #[tokio::test]\n    async fn test_boot_to_shutdown() {\n        let (tx, rx) = mpsc::channel(8);\n        let cfg = NodeConfig {\n            is_validator: false,\n            ..Default::default()\n        };\n        let _handle = NodeStateMachine::spawn(NodeContext::new(cfg, tx.clone()), rx);\n\n        // Ask the machine to shutdown\n        tx.send(NodeEvent::Shutdown).await.unwrap();\n\n        // Wait a short time to ensure completion\n        // we don't assert on output; test just ensures no panic.\n        timeout(Duration::from_secs(1), async { tokio::task::yield_now().await })\n            .await\n            .unwrap();\n    }\n}\n```","numLines":443,"startLine":1,"totalLines":443}}}

CanvasChain Symphony – Minting Service\n     5→//! --------------------------------------\n     6→//! Factory & Strategy pattern–powered NFT minting logic.\n     7→//! Creates multilayer NFTs, signs mint payloads with a pluggable crypto\n     8→//! backend and broadcasts events onto the internal event-bus.\n     9→//!\n    10→//! This file purposefully hides transport concerns (gRPC, HTTP, etc.); the\n    11→//! factory is a pure domain component that can be wired into whatever\n    12→//! outer-layer the micro-service exposes.\n    13→\n    14→use std::collections::HashMap;\n    15→use std::sync::Arc;\n    16→use std::time::{SystemTime, UNIX_EPOCH};\n    17→\n    18→use async_trait::async_trait;\n    19→use serde::{Deserialize, Serialize};\n    20→use tokio::sync::RwLock;\n    21→use tracing::{debug, error, info, instrument};\n    22→use uuid::Uuid;\n    23→\n    24→use thiserror::Error;\n    25→\n    26→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    27→/// Domain Types\n    28→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    29→\n    30→/// Human-readable, multi-layer NFT metadata sent by the client.\n    31→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    32→pub struct MintRequest {\n    33→    /// Wallet address of the creator in bech32 / hex format (depends on chain).\n    34→    pub creator_wallet: String,\n    35→    /// An ordered list of media layers that compose the artwork.\n    36→    pub layers: Vec<NftLayer>,\n    37→    /// Royalty basis-points (1/100 of a percent).\n    38→    pub royalty_bps: u16,\n    39→    /// Arbitrary on-chain attributes (e.g. color_palette → “pastel”).\n    40→    pub attributes: HashMap<String, String>,\n    41→    /// Number used once to avoid replay attacks.\n    42→    pub nonce: u64,\n    43→}\n    44→\n    45→/// Individual layer of the final NFT.\n    46→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    47→pub struct NftLayer {\n    48→    pub id: u32,\n    49→    /// IPFS / Arweave / S3 URI\n    50→    pub uri: String,\n    51→}\n    52→\n    53→/// Normalised payload that the chain client can send to the runtime.\n    54→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    55→pub struct MintPayload {\n    56→    pub idempotency_key: Uuid,\n    57→    pub creator_wallet: String,\n    58→    pub metadata_cid: String,\n    59→    pub royalty_bps: u16,\n    60→    pub signature: Vec<u8>,\n    61→}\n    62→\n    63→/// Successful on-chain minting result.\n    64→#[derive(Debug, Clone, Serialize, Deserialize)]\n    65→pub struct NftReceipt {\n    66→    pub token_id: u64,\n    67→    pub tx_hash: String,\n    68→    pub signature: Vec<u8>,\n    69→}\n    70→\n    71→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    72→/// Error Handling\n    73→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n    74→\n    75→#[derive(Error, Debug)]\n    76→pub enum FactoryError {\n    77→    #[error(\"invalid royalty basis points: {0}\")]\n    78→    InvalidRoyalty(u16),\n    79→    #[error(\"mint payload validation error: {0}\")]\n    80→    Validation(String),\n    81→    #[error(\"crypto error: {0}\")]\n    82→    Crypto(#[from] CryptoError),\n    83→    #[error(\"blockchain client error: {0}\")]\n    84→    Blockchain(#[from] BlockchainError),\n    85→    #[error(\"event bus error: {0}\")]\n    86→    EventBus(#[from] EventBusError),\n    87→}\n    88→\n    89→#[derive(Error, Debug)]\n    90→pub enum CryptoError {\n    91→    #[error(\"unsupported curve\")]\n    92→    UnsupportedCurve,\n    93→    #[error(\"signature generation failed\")]\n    94→    SignFailure,\n    95→}\n    96→\n    97→#[derive(Error, Debug)]\n    98→pub enum BlockchainError {\n    99→    #[error(\"node unreachable\")]\n   100→    NodeUnreachable,\n   101→    #[error(\"transaction rejected: {0}\")]\n   102→    TransactionRejected(String),\n   103→}\n   104→\n   105→#[derive(Error, Debug)]\n   106→pub enum EventBusError {\n   107→    #[error(\"publish failed\")]\n   108→    PublishFailed,\n   109→}\n   110→\n   111→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   112→/// Traits (Strategy / Port abstractions)\n   113→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   114→\n   115→/// Unified crypto-signer strategy so we can swap Ed25519, BLS, PQ, etc.\n   116→#[async_trait]\n   117→pub trait CryptoSigner: Send + Sync {\n   118→    /// ‘Human’ name of the algorithm (ed25519, bls12381, falcon, …).\n   119→    fn algorithm(&self) -> &'static str;\n   120→\n   121→    /// Public identifier derived from the secret key (hex / bech32).\n   122→    fn public_key(&self) -> String;\n   123→\n   124→    /// Sign arbitrary message bytes.\n   125→    async fn sign(&self, message: &[u8]) -> Result<Vec<u8>, CryptoError>;\n   126→}\n   127→\n   128→/// Port for whatever chain client we’re using (substrate, cosmos, homework-chain).\n   129→#[async_trait]\n   130→pub trait BlockchainClient: Send + Sync {\n   131→    async fn submit_mint(&self, payload: MintPayload) -> Result<String, BlockchainError>;\n   132→    async fn latest_token_id(&self) -> Result<u64, BlockchainError>;\n   133→}\n   134→\n   135→/// Simplistic event bus publisher (fan-out / kafka / nats / anything).\n   136→#[async_trait]\n   137→pub trait EventPublisher: Send + Sync {\n   138→    async fn publish<M: Serialize + Send + Sync>(&self, topic: &str, msg: &M) -> Result<(), EventBusError>;\n   139→}\n   140→\n   141→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   142→/// NFT Factory (Concrete implementation of the Factory pattern)\n   143→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   144→\n   145→/// Shared reference-counted pointer to immutable factory configuration.\n   146→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\n   147→pub struct FactoryConfig {\n   148→    /// Maximum allowed royalty (bps). Prevents creator mistakes.\n   149→    pub max_royalty_bps: u16,\n   150→    /// Event bus topic for successful mints.\n   151→    pub event_topic_success: String,\n   152→    /// Event bus topic for failed mints.\n   153→    pub event_topic_failure: String,\n   154→}\n   155→\n   156→pub struct NftFactory<S, C, E>\n   157→where\n   158→    S: CryptoSigner,\n   159→    C: BlockchainClient,\n   160→    E: EventPublisher,\n   161→{\n   162→    signer: Arc<S>,\n   163→    chain: Arc<C>,\n   164→    bus: Arc<E>,\n   165→    config: Arc<FactoryConfig>,\n   166→    // optionally keep some internal state (metrics, counters, etc.)\n   167→    minted_counter: RwLock<u64>,\n   168→}\n   169→\n   170→impl<S, C, E> NftFactory<S, C, E>\n   171→where\n   172→    S: CryptoSigner + 'static,\n   173→    C: BlockchainClient + 'static,\n   174→    E: EventPublisher + 'static,\n   175→{\n   176→    pub fn new(signer: Arc<S>, chain: Arc<C>, bus: Arc<E>, config: FactoryConfig) -> Self {\n   177→        Self {\n   178→            signer,\n   179→            chain,\n   180→            bus,\n   181→            config: Arc::new(config),\n   182→            minted_counter: RwLock::new(0),\n   183→        }\n   184→    }\n   185→\n   186→    /// Validate, sign, submit and broadcast the mint request.\n   187→    #[instrument(skip_all, level = \"info\", fields(creator = %req.creator_wallet))]\n   188→    pub async fn mint_nft(&self, req: MintRequest) -> Result<NftReceipt, FactoryError> {\n   189→        self.validate_request(&req)?;\n   190→\n   191→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   192→        // 1. Serialize metadata to JSON; in reality we would push to IPFS/Arweave\n   193→        // and obtain a CID, we’ll fake it by hashing the JSON.\n   194→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   195→        let metadata_json = serde_json::to_vec(&req)\n   196→            .map_err(|e| FactoryError::Validation(format!(\"metadata serialization: {e}\")))?;\n   197→\n   198→        let metadata_cid = hex::encode(blake3::hash(&metadata_json).as_bytes());\n   199→\n   200→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   201→        // 2. Build chain payload and sign it with the selected algorithm.\n   202→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   203→        let idempotency_key = Uuid::new_v4();\n   204→        let mut payload_bytes = Vec::new();\n   205→        payload_bytes.extend_from_slice(idempotency_key.as_bytes());\n   206→        payload_bytes.extend_from_slice(metadata_cid.as_bytes());\n   207→        payload_bytes.extend_from_slice(&req.royalty_bps.to_le_bytes());\n   208→        payload_bytes.extend_from_slice(req.creator_wallet.as_bytes());\n   209→        payload_bytes.extend_from_slice(&req.nonce.to_le_bytes());\n   210→\n   211→        let signature = self.signer.sign(&payload_bytes).await?;\n   212→\n   213→        let payload = MintPayload {\n   214→            idempotency_key,\n   215→            creator_wallet: req.creator_wallet.clone(),\n   216→            metadata_cid,\n   217→            royalty_bps: req.royalty_bps,\n   218→            signature: signature.clone(),\n   219→        };\n   220→\n   221→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   222→        // 3. Submit mint extrinsic / transaction to the chain.\n   223→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   224→        let tx_hash = match self.chain.submit_mint(payload.clone()).await {\n   225→            Ok(h) => h,\n   226→            Err(e) => {\n   227→                // fire & forget failure event\n   228→                let _ = self\n   229→                    .bus\n   230→                    .publish(\n   231→                        &self.config.event_topic_failure,\n   232→                        &MintFailureEvent::from_request(&req, &format!(\"{e}\")),\n   233→                    )\n   234→                    .await;\n   235→                return Err(e.into());\n   236→            }\n   237→        };\n   238→\n   239→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   240→        // 4. Retrieve token ID (optimistic assumption we mint sequentially).\n   241→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   242→        let token_id = self.chain.latest_token_id().await?;\n   243→\n   244→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   245→        // 5. Bump metrics & broadcast success event.\n   246→        // ---------------------------------------------------------------------\n   247→        {\n   248→            let mut counter = self.minted_counter.write().await;\n   249→            *counter += 1;\n   250→        }\n   251→\n   252→        let receipt = NftReceipt {\n   253→            token_id,\n   254→            tx_hash: tx_hash.clone(),\n   255→            signature,\n   256→        };\n   257→\n   258→        self.bus\n   259→            .publish(&self.config.event_topic_success, &receipt)\n   260→            .await?;\n   261→\n   262→        info!(token_id, tx_hash, \"NFT minted successfully\");\n   263→        Ok(receipt)\n   264→    }\n   265→\n   266→    // -------------------\n   267→    // internal helpers\n   268→    // -------------------\n   269→    fn validate_request(&self, req: &MintRequest) -> Result<(), FactoryError> {\n   270→        if req.royalty_bps > self.config.max_royalty_bps {\n   271→            return Err(FactoryError::InvalidRoyalty(req.royalty_bps));\n   272→        }\n   273→        if req.layers.is_empty() {\n   274→            return Err(FactoryError::Validation(\"at least one layer required\".into()));\n   275→        }\n   276→        if req.creator_wallet.trim().is_empty() {\n   277→            return Err(FactoryError::Validation(\"creator_wallet is empty\".into()));\n   278→        }\n   279→        Ok(())\n   280→    }\n   281→}\n   282→\n   283→/// ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------\n   284→/// Event Definitions\n   285→///
